Capítulo XX

El bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, the Buddha addressed Mahāsthāmaprāpta Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “You should now know! If bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, or upāsikās who uphold the Lotus Sūtra are reviled, abused, or slandered by anyone with an evil mouth, they incur great sinful retribution, as spoken before; the merits attained by the upholders are as spoken just now: the purity of eyes, ears, nose, tongue, body, and mind.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! In the distant past, immeasurable, boundless, inconceivable incalculable kalpas ago, there was a Buddha named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. His kalpa was named Vinirbhoga, and his land was named Mahāsaṃbhava.

That Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha, in that world, expounded the Dharma for devas, humans, and asuras: for those seeking to be Śrāvakas, expounding the Dharma responding to the Four Truths, to cross beyond birth, old age, sickness, and death, to ultimate nirvāṇa; for those seeking to be Pratyekabuddhas, expounding the Dharma responding to the Twelve Links of Dependent Origination; for Bodhisattvas, on account of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, expounding the Dharma responding to the Six Pāramitās, to ultimate Buddha wisdom.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! The lifespan of this Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha was forty thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands of kalpas; the kalpas of the Correct Dharma abiding in the world were equal to the dust motes of a single Jambudvīpa; the kalpas of the Semblance Dharma abiding were equal to the dust motes of the four continents. That Buddha, having benefited wandering beings, passed into extinction.

After the Correct Dharma and Semblance Dharma were entirely exhausted, in that land another Buddha appeared, also named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. In this manner successively, there were twenty thousand billion Buddhas, all with the same name.

After the initial Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata passed into extinction, and after the Correct Dharma ended, during the Semblance Dharma, bhikṣus of arrogant pride possessed great power and influence. At that time, there was a Bodhisattva bhikṣu named Sadāparibhūta.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! For what causal condition was he named Sadāparibhūta? Whenever this bhikṣu saw anyone—whether bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, or upāsikā—he bowed down to them in homage, praised them, and spoke these words: ‘I deeply revere you all; I dare not slight or despise you. For what reason? You all practice the Bodhisattva path and will become Buddhas.’

And this bhikṣu did not specifically read or recite the scriptures, but merely practiced bowing in homage; even seeing the fourfold assembly from afar, he would intentionally go to bow in homage, praise them, and speak these words: ‘I dare not slight you; you will all become Buddhas.’

Among the fourfold assembly, there were those who gave rise to anger and impure minds, reviling him with evil mouths, saying: ‘Where does this ignorant bhikṣu come from? Claiming of himself: “I do not slight you,” and conferring prophecies upon us that we will become Buddhas! We have no use for such false prophecies.’

Passing through many years in this manner, constantly reviled, he gave rise to no anger, constantly speaking: ‘You will become Buddhas.’ When he spoke these words, people sometimes struck him with sticks, wood, tiles, or stones; fleeing and standing far away, he still proclaimed in a loud voice: ‘I dare not slight you; you will all become Buddhas!’

Because he constantly spoke these words, the bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās of arrogant pride named him Sadāparibhūta.

When this bhikṣu approached death, in open space he heard the twenty thousand billion verses of the Lotus Sūtra previously expounded by Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha, fully able to receive and uphold them all, immediately attaining the purity of the eye faculty, and the purity of ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind faculties as spoken above. Having attained the purity of these six faculties, his lifespan was lengthened by two hundred million billion inconceivable years, and he expounded this Lotus Sūtra widely for people.

At that time, the fourfold assembly of arrogant pride—bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās—who had despised this person and given him the name Sadāparibhūta, seeing that he had attained great spiritual powers, the power of joyful and unhindered eloquence, and the power of great wholesome tranquility, hearing what he expounded, all believed, submitted, and followed him.

This Bodhisattva further transformed thousands of billions of multitudes, causing them to abide in anuttarasamyaksambodhi. After his life ended, he encountered two thousand billion Buddhas, all named Candrasūryapradīpa; within their Dharma, he expounded this Lotus Sūtra.

By this causal condition, he further encountered two thousand billion Buddhas, all named Meghasvaradīparāja; within the Dharma of these Buddhas, receiving, upholding, reading, reciting, and expounding this scripture for the fourfold assembly, he attained this purity of the ordinary eye, and the purity of ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind faculties, expounding the Dharma amidst the fourfold assembly with mind free of fear.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! This Bodhisattva Mahāsattva Sadāparibhūta made offerings to such numerous Buddhas as these, revering, honoring, praising them, and planting roots of virtue; afterward, he further encountered thousands of billions of Buddhas, also expounding this scripture within the Dharma of those Buddhas, his merits accomplished, destined to become a Buddha.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! What do you think: could the Bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta at that time have been someone else? It was I myself. If in past lives I had not received, upheld, read, recited, and expounded this scripture for others, I would not have been able swiftly to attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi. Because under past Buddhas I received, upheld, read, recited, and expounded this scripture for others, I swiftly attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! At that time, the fourfold assembly—bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās—because with angry minds they despised and scorned me, for two hundred billion kalpas never encountered a Buddha, never heard the Dharma, and never saw the Saṅgha; for a thousand kalpas in the Avīci Hell they underwent great suffering and affliction. Having exhausted this transgression, they again encountered Bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta, who taught and transformed them in anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta, what do you think: could those in the fourfold assembly at that time who constantly despised this Bodhisattva have been someone else? They are the five hundred Bodhisattvas in this assembly, such as Bhadrapāla; the five hundred bhikṣus, such as Siṃhacandra; and the five hundred upāsakas, such as Sugatacetanā, who are all irreversible from anuttarasamyaksambodhi!

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! You should know that this Lotus Sūtra greatly benefits all Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, able to cause them to arrive at anuttarasamyaksambodhi. Therefore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, should constantly receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, and copy this scripture.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

In the past there was a Buddha,

Named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja,

Of immeasurable spiritual wisdom,

Guiding all beings;

Devas, humans, Nagas, and spirits,

Together made offerings to him.

After this Buddha passed into extinction,

When the Dharma was about to end,

There was a Bodhisattva,

Named Sadāparibhūta.

At that time the fourfold assembly,

Were attached to dharmas.

Bodhisattva Paribhūta,

Went to where they were,

And addressed them:

“I do not slight you;

You practice the path,

And will all become Buddhas.”

All the people, hearing this,

Slighted, defamed, and reviled him;

Bodhisattva Paribhūta,

Was able to endure it.

When his transgressions were exhausted,

Approaching the end of his life,

He attained hearing this scripture,

And his six faculties were purified.

Through spiritual powers,

His lifespan was lengthened,

And again for all people,

He widely expounded this scripture.

The multitudes attached to dharmas,

All received the Bodhisattva’s

Teaching, transformation, and accomplishment,

Causing them to abide in the Buddha path.

When Paribhūta’s life ended,

He encountered countless Buddhas;

Because of expounding this scripture,

He attained immeasurable merit;

Gradually fulfilling virtues,

He swiftly accomplished the Buddha path.

The Paribhūta of that time,

Was none other than myself.

The fourfold assembly at that time,

Who were attached to dharmas,

Hearing Paribhūta say:

“You will become Buddhas”;

By this causal condition,

Encountered countless Buddhas.

The assembly of five hundred Bodhisattvas

In this gathering,

Along with the fourfold assembly,

Pure faithful men and women,

Now standing before me,

Listening to the Dharma, are them.

I in past lives,

Urged these people,

To hear and receive this scripture,

The foremost Dharma;

Opening and teaching people,

Causing them to abide in nirvāṇa;

Life after life receiving and upholding

Such a scripture as this.

Throughout tens of billions of kalpas,

Down to the inconceivable,

Only then at certain times does one hear

This Lotus Sūtra.

Throughout tens of billions of kalpas,

Down to the inconceivable,

The Buddhas, World-Honored Ones,

Only at certain times expound this scripture.

Therefore practitioners,

After the Buddha’s extinction,

Hearing such a scripture as this,

Should not give rise to doubt;

They should single-mindedly

Widely expound this scripture,

Life after life encountering Buddhas,

Swiftly accomplishing the Buddha path.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XXI

Los poderes espirituales del Tathāgata

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a thousand worlds who had welled forth from the earth, all before the Buddha single-mindedly joined their palms, gazed upon the venerable countenance, and addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! After the Buddha’s extinction, in the lands where the World-Honored One’s emanation bodies dwell and in the places where they pass into extinction, we will widely expound this scripture. For what reason? We ourselves wish to attain this genuine, pure, great Dharma, to receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, and make offerings to it.”

Then the World-Honored One, before Mañjuśrī and immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas who had long dwelled in the Sahā world, as well as bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans—before the entire assembly, manifested great spiritual powers: extending his broad and long tongue upward to the Brahmā Heaven, and from all pores emitted light of immeasurable, countless colors, universally illuminating worlds of the ten directions.

The Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath the jewel trees also did likewise, extending broad and long tongues and emitting immeasurable light.

When Śākyamuni Buddha and the Buddhas beneath the jewel trees manifested their spiritual powers, a full hundred thousand years elapsed, after which they drew back their tongues. Simultaneously they cleared their throats and snapped their fingers together; these two sounds pervaded worlds of the Buddhas in the ten directions, and the earth shook in six ways.

The wandering beings within them—devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans—through the Buddha’s spiritual power, all saw the Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of jewel trees in this Sahā world; and saw Śākyamuni Buddha together with Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata seated upon the lion seat within the jewel stūpa; and saw immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas and the fourfold assembly reverently surrounding Śākyamuni Buddha.

Having seen this, they all rejoiced greatly, attaining what they had never had before.

Immediately the devas in open space proclaimed in loud voices: “Across these immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable worlds, there is a land named Sahā; within it is a Buddha named Śākyamuni, who now for the sake of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas expounds the Great Vehicle sūtra named the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas. You should rejoice in accord with a deep mind, and should bow down and make offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha!”

All those wandering beings, hearing the voices in open space, joined their palms toward the Sahā world and said: “Homage to Śākyamuni Buddha! Homage to Śākyamuni Buddha!”

With various flowers, incense, necklaces, banners, canopies, personal ornaments, and rare treasures, they all scattered them together toward the Sahā world from afar. The scattered objects, arriving from the ten directions like gathering clouds, transformed into a jewel canopy, covering all the Buddhas here above. At that time, worlds of the ten directions penetrated without hindrance, like a single Buddha land.

Then the Buddha addressed Viśiṣṭacāritra and the assembly of Bodhisattvas: “The spiritual powers of the Buddhas are immeasurable, boundless, and inconceivable like this. If I, through these spiritual powers, throughout immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, for the sake of entrustment, were to speak of the merits of this scripture, I could not exhaust them.

In essence: all dharmas possessed by the Tathāgata, all sovereign spiritual powers of the Tathāgata, all secret essential treasuries of the Tathāgata, and all profound matters of the Tathāgata are proclaimed and revealed in this scripture.

Therefore, you all, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, should single-mindedly receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, and practice it as taught. In whatever land, if there are those who receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, or practice it as taught, or wherever copies of the scripture reside—whether in a garden, in a forest, beneath a tree, in a monastery, in a white-robed layman’s house, in a palace hall, or in a mountain valley or wilderness—in all these places one should erect a stūpa and make offerings.

For what reason? You should know that this place is the place of awakening: here the Buddhas attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi, here the Buddhas turned the wheel of Dharma, and here the Buddhas entered parinirvāṇa.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

The Buddhas, saviors of the world,

Abiding in great spiritual powers,

To delight wandering beings,

Manifest immeasurable spiritual powers;

Their tongues reach the Brahmā Heaven,

Their bodies emit countless lights;

For those who seek the Buddha path,

They manifest these rare matters.

The sound of the Buddhas clearing their throats,

And the sound of snapping their fingers,

Universally heard in lands of the ten directions,

The earth shaking in six ways.

Because after the Buddha’s extinction,

One can uphold this scripture,

All the Buddhas rejoice,

Manifesting immeasurable spiritual powers.

To entrust this scripture,

Praising those who receive and uphold it,

Throughout immeasurable kalpas,

Still it cannot be exhausted.

The merits of this person,

Are boundless and without end,

Like open space in the ten directions,

Whose limits cannot be found.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Have already seen me,

And have seen Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

And all the emanation Buddhas;

They also see me today,

Teaching and transforming Bodhisattvas.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Cause me and my emanation bodies,

And the extinct Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

To completely rejoice.

Present Buddhas of the ten directions,

As well as past and future Buddhas,

They also behold and make offerings to,

And cause to attain joy.

What the Buddhas at the place of awakening

Attained as the secret essential Dharma,

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Before long will also attain.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Regarding the meanings of all dharmas,

Their names and expressions,

Delight in speaking without end,

Like wind in open space,

Completely free of hindrances.

After the Tathāgata’s extinction,

Knowing the scriptures spoken by the Buddha,

Their causal conditions and sequences,

Expounding according to the meaning as it is;

Like the light of the sun and moon,

Able to dispel all darkness.

This person traveling through the world,

Can extinguish the darkness of wandering beings,

Teaching immeasurable Bodhisattvas,

Ultimately to abide in the One Vehicle.

Therefore those who possess wisdom,

Hearing this benefit of merit,

After my extinction,

Should receive and uphold this scripture;

This person regarding the Buddha path,

Definitive and without doubt!

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XXII

La encomienda

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Śākyamuni Buddha arose from the Dharma seat, manifested great spiritual powers, and with his right hand rubbed the crowns of immeasurable Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, speaking these words:

“Throughout immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, I cultivated this difficult-to-attain Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; I now entrust it to you all. You should single-mindedly propagate this Dharma, widely causing it to increase.”

In this manner three times he rubbed the crowns of the Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, speaking these words: “Throughout immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, I cultivated this difficult-to-attain Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; I now entrust it to you all. You should receive, uphold, read, recite, and widely proclaim this Dharma, causing all wandering beings universally to hear and know it.

For what reason? The Tathāgata possesses great love and compassion, free of stinginess, and free of fear; he is able to give to wandering beings the Buddha’s wisdom, the Tathāgata’s wisdom, and spontaneous wisdom; the Tathāgata is the great donor to all wandering beings. You too should follow and learn the Tathāgata’s Dharma, not giving rise to stinginess.

In future ages, if there is a good man or good woman who believes in the Tathāgata’s wisdom, you should expound this Lotus Sūtra for them, causing them to hear and know it, in order to cause that person to attain the Buddha’s wisdom. If there are wandering beings who do not believe and accept it, you should instruct, benefit, and delight them within other deep dharmas of the Tathāgata. If you can act like this, you will have repaid the kindness of all Buddhas.”

At that time, the Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, hearing the Buddha speak thus, were all filled with great joy pervading their bodies; with doubled reverence, bowing, lowering their heads, and joining their palms toward the Buddha, they simultaneously raised their voices, saying:

“As the World-Honored One commands, we will fully practice it. Yes, World-Honored One! We pray you will have no anxiety.”

The assembly of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, in this manner three times, simultaneously raised their voices, saying: “As the World-Honored One commands, we will fully practice it. Yes, World-Honored One! We pray you will have no anxiety.”

Then Śākyamuni Buddha caused all the emanation Buddhas who had arrived from the ten directions to return each to their native lands, speaking these words: “May the Buddhas each dwell in peace as they please; and the stūpa of Prabhūtaratna Buddha may return as before.”

When these words were spoken, the immeasurable emanation Buddhas of the ten directions seated upon lion seats beneath jewel trees, Prabhūtaratna Buddha, Viśiṣṭacāritra and the boundless incalculable assembly of Bodhisattvas, Śāriputra and the fourfold assembly of Śrāvakas, as well as all devas, humans, and asuras in the world, hearing what the Buddha expounded, all rejoiced greatly.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo XXIII

Las acciones pasadas del bodhisattva Bhaiṣajyarāja

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! How does Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva travel in the Sahā world? World-Honored One! How many hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of difficult and bitter practices has this Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva performed? Excellent, World-Honored One! Pray explain a little.”

All devas, Nagas, spirits, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, non-humans, the Bodhisattvas who had arrived from other lands, and this assembly of Śrāvakas, hearing this, all rejoiced.

Then the Buddha addressed Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva: “In the distant past, kalpas equal to immeasurable Ganges sands ago, there was a Buddha named Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān.

That Buddha had eighty billion great Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas and a great assembly of Śrāvakas equal to the sands of seventy-two Ganges rivers. The Buddha’s lifespan was forty-two thousand kalpas, and the lifespans of the Bodhisattvas were also equal. That land had no women, hells, hungry ghosts, animals, asuras, or tribulations; the ground was flat as the palm of a hand, made of lapis lazuli, adorned with jewel trees, covered with jewel curtains, draped with jewel flower banners; jewel vases and censers pervaded the realm; seven-jeweled platforms stood, one tree to each platform, the trees separated from the platforms by the distance of an arrow’s flight.

Beneath all these jewel trees, Bodhisattvas and Śrāvakas sat. Upon each jewel platform, tens of billions of devas made heavenly music, singing praises to the Buddha as offerings. At that time, that Buddha expounded the Lotus Sūtra for Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, the assembly of Bodhisattvas, and the assembly of Śrāvakas.

This Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva delighted in practicing austerities. Within the Dharma of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, he vigorously practiced walking meditation, single-mindedly seeking Buddhahood; fulfilling twelve thousand years, he attained the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies. Having attained this samādhi, his heart rejoiced greatly, thinking: ‘My attaining the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies is entirely through the power of hearing the Lotus Sūtra; I should now make offerings to Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha and the Lotus Sūtra.’

Immediately he entered this samādhi, and from open space rained down māndārava flowers, mahā-māndārava flowers, and fine powdered hard black sandalwood, filling open space, descending like clouds; he also rained the sandalwood incense of this shore of the ocean—six grains of this incense being worth the Sahā world—to make offerings to the Buddha.

Having made this offering, he arose from samādhi and thought to himself: ‘Although I have made offerings to the Buddha through spiritual powers, it is not equal to offering my own body.’

Immediately he ingested various fragrances—sandalwood, frankincense, turuṣka, priyaṅgu, aloeswood, and resin incense—and drank the scented oil of campaka flowers. Fulfilling twelve hundred years, he anointed his body with scented oil, and before Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, wrapped his body in heavenly jewel robes, poured scented oil over himself, and through the power of spiritual vows burned his own body. Its light universally illuminated worlds equal to the sands of eighty billion Ganges rivers.

The Buddhas within them simultaneously praised: ‘Excellent, excellent! Good son! This is genuine vigor; this is named making offerings of the genuine Dharma to the Tathāgata. If with flowers, incense, necklaces, burning incense, powdered incense, paste incense, heavenly silk banners, canopies, and the sandalwood incense of this shore of the ocean, one were to make offerings with such various things, it could not equal this; even if giving countries, cities, wives, and children, it could not equal this. Good son! This is named the foremost giving; among all givings, it is supreme and unexcelled, because one makes offerings of the Dharma to the Tathāgatas!’ Having spoken these words, they became silent.

His body burned with fire for twelve hundred years; only after this did his body become exhausted.

Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, having made such a Dharma offering, after his life ended was reborn in the land of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha, in the home of King Pure Virtue, sitting cross-legged, suddenly born by transformation. Immediately he spoke gāthās for his father:

Great King, you should now know:

Practicing walking meditation in that place,

Immediately I attained

The samādhi of manifesting all bodies.

Diligently practicing great vigor,

I abandoned the body I cherished,

Making offerings to the World-Honored One,

To seek unexcelled wisdom.

Having spoken these gāthās, he addressed his father: ‘Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha is even now present. Earlier, having made offerings to the Buddha, I attained the dhāraṇī of understanding the languages of all wandering beings, and further heard this Lotus Sūtra in hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of kaṅkaras, bimbaras, and akṣobhyas of verses. Great King! I should now return to make offerings to this Buddha.’

Having spoken thus, he sat upon a seven-jeweled platform, ascended into open space to the height of seven tāla trees, went to where the Buddha was, bowed with his head touching the Buddha’s feet, joined his ten fingers, and praised the Buddha in gāthās:

Your countenance is extraordinarily wondrous,

Your light illuminates the ten directions;

I recently made offerings to you,

And now return in person to behold you.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, having spoken these gāthās, addressed the Buddha: ‘World-Honored One! The World-Honored One is still in the world!’

At that time, Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha addressed Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva: ‘Good son! The time of my nirvāṇa has arrived, the time of extinction is here; you may arrange a seat; tonight I will enter parinirvāṇa.’

He further commanded Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva: ‘Good son! I entrust the Buddha-Dharma to you and all the Bodhisattva great disciples, along with the Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; and I entrust to you the seven-jeweled worlds of the great three-thousand-fold worlds, with all their jewel trees, jewel platforms, and attendant devas. After my extinction, whatever relics there are I also entrust to you; you should cause them to be distributed, widely setting forth offerings, erecting several thousand stūpas.’

In this manner Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha commanded Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, and in the latter part of the night entered nirvāṇa.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, seeing the Buddha pass into extinction, grieved, lamented, and longed for the Buddha. Immediately with sandalwood from this shore of the ocean as a pyre, he made offerings to the Buddha’s body, and cremated it.

After the fire died out, he gathered the relics, made eighty-four thousand precious urns, and erected eighty-four thousand stūpas, three worlds in height, with adorned spires, hanging banners, canopies, and jewel bells.

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva thought to himself again: ‘Although I have made these offerings, my mind is not yet satisfied; I should now make further offerings to the relics.’

He said to the Bodhisattva great disciples, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, and the entire assembly: ‘You should all be single-mindedly mindful; I now make offerings to the relics of Candrasūryavimalaprabhāsaśrī Buddha.’

Having spoken these words, before the eighty-four thousand stūpas, he burned his arm adorned with a hundred merits for seventy-two thousand years as an offering, causing countless multitudes seeking to be Śrāvakas and immeasurable incalculable people to generate the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, all enabling them to dwell in the samādhi of manifesting all physical bodies.

At that time, the Bodhisattvas, devas, humans, and asuras, seeing him without an arm, grieved, sorrowed, and lamented, saying: ‘This Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva is our teacher who taught and transformed us; yet now he has burned his arm, his body incomplete!’

Then Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva, amidst the great assembly, made this vow: ‘Having cast off both arms, I will certainly attain the golden body of a Buddha; if this is genuine and not false, may both my arms be restored as before!’

Having made this vow, they were naturally restored as before, brought about by the deep purity of this Bodhisattva’s merit and wisdom. At that moment, the great three-thousand-fold worlds shook in six ways, heaven rained down jewel flowers, and all humans and devas attained what they had never had before.”

The Buddha addressed Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva: “What do you think: could Sarvasattvapriyadarśana Bodhisattva have been someone else? It was Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva himself. The instances of his abandoning his body in giving are immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers like this.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If someone generates the resolve wishing to attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi, and is able to burn a finger or even a single toe as an offering to a Buddha stūpa, it surpasses offering countries, cities, wives, children, and the mountains, forests, rivers, ponds, and treasures of the great three-thousand-fold worlds.

If someone fills the great three-thousand-fold worlds with the seven treasures to make offerings to the Buddha, great Bodhisattvas, Pratyekabuddhas, and arhats, the merit attained by that person does not equal receiving and upholding this Lotus Sūtra, down to a single four-line gāthā; its merit is foremost!

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! Just as among all streams, rivers, and waters, the ocean is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among scriptures expounded by the Tathāgatas, it is the deepest and greatest.

Just as among earthen mountains, black mountains, the Small Iron Ring Mountains, the Great Iron Ring Mountains, and the ten jewel mountains, Mount Sumeru is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among all scriptures, it stands supreme.

Just as among the stars, the Moon Deva is foremost; this Lotus Sūtra is also like this: among thousands of billions of scriptural dharmas, it illuminates most brightly.

Just as the Sun Deva can dispel all darkness; this scripture is also like this: able to shatter all darkness of unwholesome things.

Just as among petty kings, the wheel-turning sage king is foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptures, it is most revered.

Just as Śakra is king among the Trāyastriṃśa devas; this scripture is also like this: the King among scriptures.

Just as the Great Brahmā King is the father of all wandering beings; this scripture is also like this: the father of all worthy sages, practitioners in training and beyond training, and those who generate the Bodhisattva resolve.

Just as among all common mortals, the srotāpanna, sakṛdāgāmin, anāgāmin, arhat, and pratyekabuddha are foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptural dharmas expounded by Tathāgatas, Bodhisattvas, or Śrāvakas, it is foremost. Those who can receive and uphold this scripture are also like this: among all wandering beings, they are foremost.

Among all Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas, the Bodhisattva is foremost; this scripture is also like this: among all scriptural dharmas, it is foremost.

Just as the Buddha is the King of all dharmas; this scripture is also like this: the King of all scriptures.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! This scripture can rescue all wandering beings; this scripture can cause all wandering beings to be free of suffering and afflictions; this scripture can greatly benefit all wandering beings, fulfilling their vows.

Like a cool, clear pond, able to satisfy all who are thirsty; like the cold finding fire, like the naked finding clothing, like merchants finding a caravan leader, like a child finding a mother, like a ferry finding a boat, like the sick finding a physician, like darkness finding a lamp, like the poor finding treasure, like subjects finding a king, like traders finding the ocean, like a torch dispelling darkness: this Lotus Sūtra is also like this, able to cause wandering beings to abandon all suffering and illness, able to untie all the bonds of birth and death.

If someone hears this Lotus Sūtra, whether copying it oneself or causing others to copy it, the merit attained, calculating it with the Buddha’s wisdom, cannot reach its limit. If copying this scriptural scroll, making offerings of flowers, incense, necklaces, burning incense, powdered incense, paste incense, banners, canopies, clothing, and various lamps—ghee lamps, oil lamps, scented oil lamps, campaka oil lamps, sumanā oil lamps, pāṭala oil lamps, vārṣikā oil lamps, and navamallikā oil lamps—the merit attained is also immeasurable.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If someone hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter, they also attain immeasurable, boundless merit. If a woman hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter and can receive and uphold it, when this female body ends, she will never receive a female body again.

If after the Tathāgata’s extinction, during the final five hundred years, a woman hears this scripture and practices as taught: dying here, she will immediately go to the Land of Joyful Ease, where Amitābha Buddha dwells surrounded by the assembly of great Bodhisattvas, born upon a jewel seat within a lotus flower, no longer afflicted by greed and lust, nor afflicted by anger and delusion, nor afflicted by pride, envy, and defilements; attaining the Bodhisattva’s spiritual superknowledges and the forbearance of non-arising dharmas.

Having attained this forbearance, her eye faculty is pure; with this pure eye faculty, she beholds Buddhas, Tathāgatas equal to the sands of seven hundred and two thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges rivers.

At that time, the Buddhas praise her from afar: ‘Excellent, excellent! Good son! That within the Dharma of Śākyamuni Buddha you were able to receive, uphold, read, recite, and contemplate this scripture, and expound it for others, the merit you attain is immeasurable and boundless; fire cannot burn it, water cannot sweep it away; your virtues, a thousand Buddhas speaking together could not exhaust. You have now shattered the thieves of Mara, destroyed the army of birth and death, and crushed all other enemies. Good son! Hundreds of thousands of Buddhas guard you together through spiritual powers; in all the world, among devas and humans, none is equal to you, except the Tathāgata; among Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, down to Bodhisattvas, none possess wisdom and dhyāna meditation equal to yours!’

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! This Bodhisattva accomplishes such power of virtue and wisdom. If someone hears this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter, and can rejoice in accord and praise its goodness, in this present life the fragrance of a blue lotus flower will constantly issue from their mouth, and the fragrance of ox-head sandalwood will constantly issue from the pores of their body; the merits attained are as spoken above.

Therefore, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! I entrust this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter to you. After my extinction, in the final five hundred years, widely proclaim and propagate it in Jambudvīpa, do not allow it to be cut off, lest evil Maras, the subjects of Mara, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, and kumbhāṇḍas gain an opening!

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! You should guard this scripture through spiritual powers. For what reason? This scripture is good medicine for the illnesses of people in Jambudvīpa. If someone is ill and hears this scripture, their illness will be eliminated, free of old age and death.

Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! If you see someone who upholds this scripture, you should take a blue lotus flower filled with powdered incense and scatter it over them as an offering. Having scattered it, have this thought: ‘This person before long will certainly take grass and sit at the place of awakening, shattering the armies of Mara, blowing the Dharma conch, striking the great Dharma drum, and liberating all wandering beings from the ocean of old age, sickness, and death.’

Therefore, those who seek the Buddha path, seeing those who uphold this scripture, should give rise to such a reverent mind as this.”

When this Former activities of Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva Chapter was expounded, eighty-four thousand Bodhisattvas attained the dhāraṇī of understanding the languages of all wandering beings.

Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, from within the jewel stūpa, praised Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña Bodhisattva: “Excellent, excellent, Nakṣatrarājasamkusumitābhijña! You have accomplished inconceivable merits, that you are able to question Śākyamuni Buddha on such matters as these, benefiting immeasurable wandering beings!”
